And whereas the diagnosis of autism has increased due to greater awareness and understanding, however, autism spectrum disorder remains a complex developmental condition

[00:48:04] that presents unique strengths, challenges, and needs for each individual. And whereas young adults on the autism spectrum have low employment rates among those with disabilities, despite their unique skills and talents. And whereas with supports like modified hiring practices, job coaching, and work opportunity tax credits, individuals with autism can thrive in the workplace, demonstrated by a 59% employment success rate after vocational rehabilitation.

[00:48:35] And whereas Fairfax County hosts exemplary organizations like Service Source and the Phillips programs, providing critical services and promoting meaningful employment for individuals with autism, particularly as they face the steep services cliff in adulthood. Now, therefore, be it resolved that the Fairfax County Board of Supervisors on behalf of all residents of Fairfax County does hereby proclaim April 2025 as Autism Acceptance Month in Fairfax County and urges all residents to show respect and compassion to those

[01:23:45] Whereas April 30th, 2025, marks the 50th anniversary of the fall of Saigon, a pivotal event that concluded the Vietnam War and led to the displacement of hundreds of thousands of Vietnamese people who fled their homeland in search of freedom.

[01:24:03] And whereas these refugees risked their lives, faced perilous journeys, and encountered numerous challenges, with many resettling in the United States, forming a large Vietnamese-American community of over 70,000 in northern Virginia, including thousands in Fairfax County.

[02:23:09] Thank you, Mr. Chairman. I have three board matters. The first one is a joint board matter with yourself, Supervisor Lusk, and Supervisor Beerman. Mr. Chairman, the Deferred Retirement Option Program, also known as DROP, is a means of providing employees who choose to work beyond their normal retirement dates the flexibility to elect to receive some of the retirement benefits in a lump sum, rather than continuing to earn additional years of retirement service and higher monthly annuities.

[02:23:40] There have been many benefits since DROP was approved and implemented to staff and the county more generally. The program was adopted in response to employee interest in added benefit flexibility and management's recognition that DROP would assist in workforce planning, given that it both encouraged employees to stay beyond their normal retirement date and provided a more definitive date of retirement for planning purposes.

[02:24:07] Given the success of DROP, I believe there are ways to enhance the program further that could include potential cost savings to the county by adding value to the sick time accrued by those in the program. As it currently stands, sick time is not paid. out once an individual either completes their three-year period that drop allows for or exits the program sooner. Specific to public safety and due to minimum staffing requirements, if an employee calls out sick while in drop, the county must pay that employee their hourly rate as well as the rate

[02:24:40] to the individual who replaces them on any given shift. The rate of the employee who is called in to work can in some instances be higher than that of who they replace. The dollar amounts can be more significant over the course of a fiscal year than a time when we face challenging fiscal realities.

[02:25:00] However, it is my belief that it is a worthwhile endeavor to examine the potential benefits of adding value to sick time accrued while in drop as another tool to lower overtime costs. Therefore, Mr. Chairman, while I recognize there are variations in the duration that individuals remain in drop, I request that the Department of Management and Budget, along with any other appropriate county agencies, develop a pilot program specific to the Fairfax County Fire and Rescue Department that allows payout of sick time accrued while in drop. Such a program should be presented at the Personnel Committee, at the Board of Supervisors, with

[02:25:37] varying options to the level of payout. And I'm happy to second that motion and add that This is a request of staff to come back with this as an option to the committee for us to vet. [03:37:16] We're here to present proposed changes to the employees' police officers and uniformed retirement systems ordinances, specifically regarding animal protective police officers. As part of the process of reclassifying the animal protective police officer positions from the uniformed retirement system to the employees' retirement system, impacted employees will be given the opportunity to join either the Department of Animal Services or the police department.

[03:37:40] In addition, current employees will have the following voluntary options regarding which retirement systems they will be members in. They can choose to remain members of the Uniformed Retirement System, leave the Uniformed Retirement System, and become members of the police officer's retirement system, or leave the Uniformed Retirement System and become members of the employee's retirement system.

[03:38:03] To enable these possible transitions, the ordinance for the three retirement systems need to be amended as noted in Attachment 1. Specifically, the amendments to the employees and police officers ordinance will allow APPOs to be members of those systems.

[03:38:18] The amendments to the uniformed ordinance will define the process for APPOs to transition to the employees or police systems or allow the APPOs who choose to remain as members of the uniformed retirement system. The cost of the member is based on calculations and assumptions made by the county's actuary, as noted in Attachment 2, meaning that the three retirement systems will be no better or worse off.

[03:38:45] Funding-wise, the county's liability for future benefit payments will not increase or decrease, and there will be no impact on the county's contribution to the three retirement systems. The three retirement systems boards of trustees have been informed of these proposed changes, and staff recommends approval.

This is a public hearing to consider implementing a food and beverage tax, which would apply to food and beverages sold ready to eat. Although the FY2026 advertised budget plan does not include a food and beverage tax, the county executive recommended that the Board of Supervisors consider such a tax as part of their deliberation on the FY2026 budget to allow discussion with the community in the context of the overall budget, particularly as approximately one-third of the revenue is projected to be generated from out-of-county residents.

[04:15:17] Virginia Code authorizes counties to impose a food and beverage tax at a rate not to exceed 6%. However, the board authorized advertisement of a rate not to exceed 4%, which sets the upper limit that the board can approve for FY2026. The board has the option to adopt a dealer discount, which would allow restaurants to retain a small portion of the tax to offset the cost of collecting the tax on the county's behalf.

[04:15:40] The board advertised a dealer discount, if implemented, at a rate no greater than 3% of the tax collected. Each 1% on the tax rate is estimated to generate $35 million in annual gross revenue before any dealer discount and before administrative expenses, totaling $2.8 million and 21 positions.

[04:15:58] Staff recommend an effective date no earlier than January 1, 2026. If implemented on that date, a half year of revenue would be realized in FY2026. I would note that while this public hearing is specific to the proposed food and beverage tax, Individuals may also provide comments on this topic during the public hearings on the budget, which will begin later this afternoon and continue over the next two days.

[09:49:28] Many of them are women of color who have committed their careers to fostering a creative and enriching educational environment for our kids.
